## Spravovaný runtime — start a vlastnictví PID — 2026-11-19

- Read-only kontrola našla jeden loopback listener, `/health` 6.1.0, `/ready=true`, režim `active` a registraci Windows Run-key bez naplánované úlohy. Nový interpret venv načetl editable balíček z pracovního checkoutu. Během ověřování se PID služby změnil. Ze shody verze ani z cesty nového interpretu nelze určit revizi všech modulů v běžícím procesu. Pro tuto etapu neproběhl zásah do služby, registrace, kapacity, modelu ani projektů.
- Skutečný izolovaný start na Windows odhalil rozdíl mezi `hub.pid` a `hub.owner.json`. HTTP worker na Windows zapisoval vlastní PID do souboru určeného launcheru. PID záznam nyní zapisuje a uklízí pouze supervisor. Cílený test před opravou zachytil přepsání při startu a po opravě potvrdil zachování záznamu i při chybě inicializace telemetrie.
- Windows Python 3.13.2: 32 testů startu, režimu a update transakce prošlo, jeden symlinkový případ se přeskočil kvůli oprávnění. Prošlo také 13 testů vlastnictví a MCP a samostatně opakovaný start. Dalších 125 testů pokrylo výměnu embedding prostoru, výběr souborů, enrichment a zápisy SQLite. Debian Python 3.13.18: 20 souvisejících testů prošlo včetně případu se symlinkem, dva čistě Windows případy se přeskočily. Při Windows běhu zůstalo všech 2 304 vstupních otisků stejných.
- Doklady v `%TEMP%\lah-runtime-rehearsal-c0z4g0qo\verified\evidence`. Instalační volba `--expected-revision` ověřuje přesný commit a vyžaduje čistý checkout. Aktuální ověření potvrzuje opravu v izolovaném runtime. Revize instalované služby, skutečné embeddings a enrichment, nový RAG dotaz a návrat capu zůstávají otevřené.

## Vyhledávání — přechod při prázdném RAG workspace — 2026-11-19

- Kontrola RAG nejprve ověří uloženou vazbu na kořen. Pro prázdný workspace bez vazby vymaže případnou starou identitu z cache a pokračuje bez načtení konfigurace přes Git. Neúplná vazba zaplněného workspace a vazba na cizí kořen se dál odmítají. Platná vazba dál čte aktuální koordinační identitu projektu. Obě cílené regrese před úpravou zachytily zbytečné volání.
- Na 16 syntetických Git repozitářích s chladnou cache a prázdným indexem klesl medián `_repo_rag_search` z 33,567 ms na 2,840 ms a p95 z 42,968 ms na 3,158 ms. Před úpravou proběhlo 17 volání Git včetně zahřívacího pokusu, po úpravě žádné. Obě měření vracela `rag_index_missing`. Jde o interní cestu na Windows. Dlouhé prodlevy živého endpointu zůstávají bez přiřazené příčiny.
- Windows ověřil 181 testů; jeden symlinkový případ se přeskočil kvůli oprávnění. Fixture grafu paměti teď dodává povinný kanonický kořen. Asynchronní integrace používá aktuální `LocalAIServices` a ověřuje model podle role i systémový prefix projektu na falešném runtime. Starý odkaz na lokální worktree už test neblokuje. ## RAG a Ollama — zrušení embeddingu ve frontě — 2026-11-19

- RAG předává zrušení Ollama scheduleru. Čekající embedding lze odebrat z fronty před spuštěním. Timeout jedné úlohy signalizuje pouze této úloze. U už rozběhnuté úlohy se dál hlásí `outcome_unknown` s identitou úlohy, dokud volající nemá potvrzené ukončení.

- Worker znovu kontroluje termín a zrušení před voláním backendu. Pokud volání nezačalo, vrátí `operation_not_started` a uvolní konkrétní přidělenou kapacitu. Selhání uvolnění zůstává viditelné přes `cleanup_unconfirmed`.

## RAG přes HTTP — zrušení po odpojení — 2026-11-19

- `/api/rag/search` nyní předává signál sledování odpojení do RAG, když klient použije `X-LocalAI-Cancel-On-Disconnect: 1`. `HubClient` hlavičku přidává automaticky u požadavku se signálem zrušení. Po odpojení již odeslaného požadavku hlásí `outcome_unknown`. Samostatný probe s MCP adaptérem zachoval i `job_id`, `scheduler_state` a `cleanup_unconfirmed` při jediném volání backendu.
- Sledování odpojení lze po dokončení požadavku ihned probudit. Medián režie na 24 vzorcích v každé variantě se socketpair a okamžitou odpovědí klesl z 50,490 ms na 0,215 ms. Výsledek měří pouze sledování spojení.
- Regrese na skutečném loopback spojení před opravou nezaznamenala zrušení na serveru. Po opravě prošlo 102 testů rozhraní HTTP, klienta a MCP a 21 testů fronty RAG. `pyflakes` prošel, 2 304 vstupů snímku bez driftu. Doklady a benchmark v `%TEMP%\lah-rag-http-disconnect-41ed9507`. ## Integrační snímek — výběr kritických testů a distribuce — 2026-11-19

- Původní pokus s `-m critical` na Windows zdědil slovo `critical` z absolutní cesty dočasné složky a vybral všech 17 960 testů. Běh byl zastaven. Klasifikace nyní používá relativní pytest node ID. Nová regrese v adresáři `critical-host` před opravou vybrala i kontrolní běžný test a po opravě pouze určený kritický test. Kontrola následného snímku vybrala 1 999 testů a vyřadila 15 965.
- Debian s Pythonem 3.13.18 ověřil 273 testů na stejných 2 304 vstupních otiscích jako Windows snímek s opravou brokeru i auditu. Pět skipů zahrnuje Windows junction, Windows Job Object a chybějící PyTorch. Linuxové testy adresářových symlinků prošly. Kontejner použil místní image, offline závislosti a vypnutou síť.
- Offline build téhož snímku vytvořil wheel 6.1.0 a sdist. Jedenáct kontrolovaných souborů modulů a konfigurace se v obou archivech byte-for-byte shodovalo se snímkem. Wheel `74fecce3a770eda0b400d0c1f9e8c438b110e1efbce38a32120909c502350caa` prošel instalací s připnutými závislostmi, `pip check` a MCP stdio smoke na Debianu s Pythonem 3.14.10. Windows offline instalace zůstává otevřená, lokální cache obsahuje jinou verzi `rpds-py` než požadovaný pin.
- Windows sonda potvrdila nesoulad identity nástroje. `shutil.which` našel Python z venv, ale holé argv `python` spustilo systémový Python bez pytestu v izolovaném prostředí. Stejný jev nastal při tvorbě ověřovacích účtenek. Absolutní cesta jako argv spustila očekávaný Python z venv. Důkazy v `%TEMP%\lah-integrated-critical-0261917c`, včetně `snapshot-run\evidence\isolated-interpreter-selection.json`, `corrected\evidence`, `executable-fixed\evidence`, `final-critical\evidence`, `linux-final` a `linux-wheel-final`.
- Kritická sada po opravě značek a před opravou brokeru skončila výsledkem 1 961 prošlo, 24 selhalo, 14 přeskočeno. Windows broker nyní při přímém spuštění použije absolutní cestu k nalezené binárce. Po opravě prošlo 267 cílených testů brokeru, účtenek a rollbacku, 16 bylo přeskočeno. Při kontrole zbylých selhání se odhalil i zastaralý statický kontrakt `POST /api/rag/search`. Audit je srovnaný s aktuálními producenty odpovědi a `tools/audit_agent_surface.py` prochází.

Po jeho zachycení se v pracovním stromu změnily `preprocess.py` a `services.py`. Samostatná sonda `structured-executable-proof.json` dokládá, že předem sestavené argv stále může spustit jiný Python.

## HUB-133 mixed-language SQLite baseline — 2026-11-19

- Windows 11, Ryzen 7 7800X3D (16 logical CPUs), Python 3.13.2, SQLite 3.45.3, CPU profile with four preprocessing workers. Source snapshot manifest SHA-256 `017fd7ff9384672babe2e6975ead6891287294a2fec94c890b735e9584acc22b`. Synthetic CZ/EN labels and PHP/Python/TSX source files. The run calls the production inventory, hash, SQLite FTS and lexical search code in isolated processes and state. The API response cache and workspace fingerprint step are bypassed.
- The default 8,000-file inventory capped a 10,000-file fixture at 8,000 rows in each of `file_refs`, `source_index` and `source_fts`. Full-index measurements set `search.max_files` to the corpus size in the isolated profile. Exact search and non-exact Python fallback still have their own 8,000-file caps.
- 10,000 files (PHP 501, Python 8,995, TSX 501, plus 3 fixture files), 327,444 source bytes and 297,445 UTF-8 bytes indexed. Inventory/hash/FTS took 10.67/7.79/15.40 s. Peak working set was 102.3 MB. DB/WAL totaled 16.72/5.84 MB before closing SQLite. The three phases transferred 246.19/248.43 MB of process reads/writes, including cached I/O.
- 10,000 files, 12 queries × 5 samples per variant. Median of the per-query p50/p95 values in ms: SQL FTS reopened 0.95/1.19, retained 0.086/0.125, production FTS candidates 20.98/27.41, exact service without/with index hints 2,010/2,331 and 2,049/2,257. These SQLite runs retain the OS cache. Raw FTS and production candidates correctly returned relevant paths in 9/11 positive cases. Exact service reached 10/11 either way with coverage complete on all 12 fixture cases. The negative control stayed empty. The earlier lexical HTTP baseline used a different entry point and execution policy.
- 100,000 files (PHP 5,001, Python 89,995, TSX 5,001, plus 3 fixture files), 3,472,924 source bytes and 3,172,925 UTF-8 bytes indexed. Inventory/hash/FTS took 125.00/132.96/384.56 s, totaling 642.52 s. Peak working set was 288.1 MB. DB/WAL reached 169.48/58.97 MB before closing SQLite. Phase totals were 3.93/4.34 GB of process reads/writes, including cached I/O.
- 100,000 files, 12 queries × 5 FTS samples per variant. Median per-query p50/p95 in ms: SQL FTS reopened 1.16/1.54, retained 0.106/0.162, production FTS candidates 215.58/235.42. Raw FTS and production candidates reached 9/11 positive cases at both sizes and kept the negative control empty. Both sizes missed `C#` and the English paraphrase for the Czech configuration guide; the 10,000-file exact service found `C#`.
- At 100,000 files, exact lexical service rejected the three selected queries with and without index hints, all six with 503, `terminal=true`, `retryable=false` and the 8,000-file scan bound. One sample per rejected variant took 14.0–15.5 s. Successful-service p50/p95 is unavailable at this size.
- Isolated revision profiling on the immutable fixtures preserved ordered paths for all 12 cases × 5 paired samples. Precomputing the revision moved median per-query p50 from 15.56 to 2.54 ms at 10,000 files and from 213.95 to 3.27 ms at 100,000 files. The fixture has no saved path hints. The experiment used the correct precomputed revision and provides no cache invalidation or concurrent-writing proof. SQLite's hash-pending query plan uses the file-reference primary key constrained by root, with `needs_hash` filtered after lookup. Late hash batches grew slower. Benchmark revisions and a selective pending-hash index independently before production changes.
- Raw evidence and harnesses are under `<user-home>\AppData\Local\Temp\lah-checkpoint-f9a2697c\hub133-indexed-run`: 10,000-file search/exact-service measurements in `indexed-10000/search.json`; 100,000-file FTS measurements in `indexed-100000/fts-search.json` and exact-service rejections in `indexed-100000/exact-service.json`; plus each corpus's `preprocessing.json` and `revision-profile.json`. I/O counters cover the benchmark process. Storage and latency are specific to this synthetic small-file workload. RAG, hybrid fusion, embeddings, inference, semantic reranking and OS-cold cache measurements remain NOT_RUN. HUB-133 remains open.

## HUB-133 batched FTS repair — 2026-11-19

- Hashing checks for stale lexical rows through the keyed SQLite tables before touching FTS. It clears stale lexical/FTS rows and repairs missing FTS rows in the same transaction. Hashing and whole-root content reuse share one uncorrelated membership lookup. Root isolation, null legacy FTS paths, existing rows, cross-worktree reuse and rollback were covered by the focused tests.
- Windows CPU benchmark reopened copies of the same 100,000-file index. Five samples per variant gave p50 of 109 → 66 ms for one unchanged file, 995 → 203 ms for 16, and 7,239 → 398 ms for 128. A single-file outlier in the first optimized process prompted a separate A/B/B/A run. With 50 samples per variant, p50 was 111.58 → 79.92 ms and p95 was 144.61 → 102.97 ms.
- Whole-root repair with one FTS row missing took 12,898 → 53 ms p50 at 10,000 files (three samples each). At 100,000 files the original hit a 30-second SQLite progress deadline, and the new median was 657 ms over three samples. The control transaction was interrupted, and the fixture row was restored separately for the final checksum comparison. Optimized process peak working set for the large run was 178.1 MB versus 152.3 MB in the bounded control.
- At both sizes the ordered file-reference, source-index and FTS content digests matched between variants. Benchmark source matched the working copy's preprocessing module. The focused suite passed 210 tests, with 17 Tree-sitter cases skipped because their parsers were unavailable. `git diff --check` passed. Raw comparisons are in `<user-home>\AppData\Local\Temp\lah-checkpoint-f9a2697c\hub133-fts-batch`. Exact large-repository search and the remaining RAG/fusion/semantic measurements are open.

## HUB-133 RAG token-overlap scaling and cancellation — 2026-11-19

- SQLite now scores the existing Python Unicode substring rule and returns only row IDs and positive scores. The bounded heap stops when it holds enough perfect matches. The selected rows are materialized within the same SQLite snapshot as ranking. Source scope, exclusion, repeated query terms, scores and tie order are preserved. Cancellation can interrupt the SQL scan. An expired FTS phase skips overlap and reports the same empty deadline outcome.
- The Windows A/B benchmark reopened copies of the same 10,000 and 100,000-chunk RAG indexes. It used the previously published deterministic test-vector fixtures and simulated query embedding unavailability. Seven cases matched exactly across entire ordered result payloads. With 16 samples per variant at 100,000 chunks, public `widget::render` p50/p95 moved from 151.11/337.91 to 80.86/115.70 ms. The negative query moved from 150.66/158.35 to 77.98/86.40 ms. Direct overlap on a common exact term with 20 perfect matches went from 230.90 to 1.26 ms p50. The mixed common and missing term still full-scans and improved from 197.56 to 141.22 ms p50.
- In fourteen uncached 10 ms probes per variant at 100,000 chunks, the original finished with ordinary fallback after 146.36 ms p50. The new path returned empty `deadline_exceeded` at 10.67 ms p50 in all fourteen probes. Some 10,000-chunk optimized probes completed normally before the deadline. These are lexical-phase timings. Cold public requests still have index revision work before retrieval.
- Windows targeted suite passed 96 tests. The regression tests cover cancellation and an inventory writer committing between ranking and row loading, along with Unicode, duplicate terms and scope. Source identity, 336 latency samples, 42 deadline probes and `git diff --check` passed. Raw reports are in `<user-home>\AppData\Local\Temp\lah-checkpoint-f9a2697c\hub133-token-overlap`. Real semantic inference, OS-cold measurements and default-budget exact search at 100,000 files remain open.

## HUB-133 RAG publication revision — 2026-11-19

- Each managed RAG publication writes a random revision token in the same SQLite transaction as the workspace generation. Bundle import now advances both after replacing the workspace. Readers use the generation and token from one snapshot. A concurrent change triggers the existing retrieval retry and updates the returned revision. Independent publications at the same generation have distinct cache identities.
- A missing token or a token for another generation keeps the manifest revision path. An existing index gains the fast path after its next publication. The code adds metadata in the existing table. Tests covered separate readers, legacy generation updates, concurrent publication and bundle crash recovery.
- On cloned Windows test indexes, both variants republished one unchanged source through removal and path indexing. Ordered public results matched. At 100,000 files with 16 samples per variant and the in-process revision memo cleared before each sample, median revision time was 259.97 → 1.55 ms and median public lexical fallback was 270.94 → 9.35 ms. Public p95 was 531.53 → 30.73 ms. The new publication performed zero manifest reads in these revision probes. Unpublished legacy replicas still scanned the manifest, 206.60 and 214.44 ms median.
- At 10,000 files, median revision time after publication was 18.90 → 1.50 ms. At a 15 ms public budget on 100,000 files, median elapsed time was 226.19 → 8.13 ms and optimized outcomes included a completed fallback and deadline expiration. The OS cache was retained. Benchmarks used the deterministic test-vector fixture, with semantic inference and reranking off.
- Windows targeted suite passed 172 tests. The opt-in live Ollama case was skipped. Measured source identity, result parity and `git diff --check` passed. Raw comparisons are in `<user-home>\AppData\Local\Temp\lah-checkpoint-f9a2697c\hub133-rag-revision`. OS-cold and installed-model evidence remain open.

## Existing-card export snapshot and resume — 2026-11-19

- Candidate listing and export now require the stored file card and its file reference to agree on the current content hash. Training rows also require the indexed source hash. Export reads the authorized index in one SQLite snapshot and avoids a redundant candidate scan. When a valid index has lost every eligible row, resume safely publishes an empty current dataset in both formats.
- Resume validates prior dataset and manifest ownership, protects the input database and its SQLite side files, and can recover a missing or outdated manifest after interrupted publication. Two stale-card regressions and an unrelated-output regression failed against the previous behavior.
- Fresh Windows checks passed 35 training scaffold and preprocessing cross-worktree cases, including a controlled concurrent WAL commit. The CLI smoke listed metadata and exported and resumed both formats on temporary SQLite state. Input database bytes were unchanged by export. Base HEAD `cc741b54dc04830900df81b5034e7f993140b3c5`. ## File-card preemption and partial batch completion — 2026-11-19

- A test reproduced a 60-second delay after ordinary foreground preemption. The card already held a usable provisional result, but the common deferral path applied the scheduler cooldown. Ordinary preemption now releases the claim for immediate resumption once foreground work permits it. Explicit delays, scheduler and missing-VRAM defaults, and uncertain outcomes keep their bounded handling. Both primary and fallback model paths use the same decision.
- A three-card test reproduced loss of a successful first generation when the second scheduler request deferred the remainder of the batch. Scheduler-backed generation now commits each usable card as it completes. The deferred and unstarted claims keep their provisional cards and the scheduler's delay. Resumption processes those pending cards without regenerating the completed one.

## Enrichment claim ownership and atomic card publication — 2026-11-19

- Three regressions reproduced an expired worker marking its successor pending, failed or complete. Each enrichment attempt now has a unique persisted claim ID and retains the content, model and pipeline identity it acquired. Completion updates the row only while that claim is still current. Expiry lets a successor take over. A result can still finish when its claim remained current through a long batch.
- A valid card, its file reference and claim completion now commit in one SQLite transaction. Injected card-write failure rolled the transaction back and kept the provisional card and current claim. Retrying with the same claim succeeded. A repeated finish after commit left the completed state intact.
- Schema initialization adds the claim ID to existing databases in place. Legacy retry times, errors and attempt counts were retained. Six concurrent initializers against one old database completed the upgrade, kept its legacy row, and granted one claim for a matching content and model. SQLite integrity passed.
- Windows validation passed 298 targeted tests, including 52 enrichment tests, the opt-in synthetic ingestion benchmark and the resumable FTS tests. `release_check.py --allow-installed` passed. The isolated `selftest.py` run passed all six checks, including HTTP health, capabilities and agent-state storage. Compile and scoped diff checks passed. The source SHA-256 was `67d8044639a3f7baafb97ad036f6e039bf53ac71ff2eb144cc29a199ec30a1f1` for `preprocess.py` and `10e8adbb0d6ac485d65753ee9162c6b34ba983aecb7d830cd71371fa0060777d` for `test_preprocessing_enrichment.py`. Managed-service activation and real model execution remain open.

## Managed installer observation deadline — 2026-11-19

- The outer updater now observes the installer for at most 45 minutes. Its staging directory, redirected output and any late installer result survive an observation timeout, invalid report or inconsistent exit status. An existing attempt directory blocks a second launch and preserves the dashboard report. A validated result or a failure before launch releases the staging directory. A timed-out or unconfirmed result is shown as unknown.
- The regression suite exercised late completion, repeated invocation, missing and malformed reports, and normal success and rollback results. The same files yielded 71 passes and one Windows symlink-privilege skip, and 72 passes on offline Debian. The first Debian run lacked Node for five dashboard tests. The final run used the verified Node 22.14.0 binary inside the container. Dashboard syntax, the release check and the 708-operation API catalog passed.

The installer later wrote a success result. A second CLI invocation was refused and the captured dashboard report remained byte-identical. Evidence is in `%TEMP%\lah-update-observer-proof-0c10gfyu`.

## Foreground code-index refresh recovery and concurrency — 2026-11-19

- A failed source read or incomplete index batch previously allowed the foreground query to cache old symbols under the new revision. The refresh now leaves the revision pending and returns a stale, degraded fallback without caching it. The code-index and deterministic query cache keys bypass entries written by the older behavior. A real CodeIndex regression proved recovery on the next request after each injected failure, including a seeded legacy cached response. This stage passed 127 Windows tests and 124 offline Debian tests, with three optional parser skips on Debian. Evidence is in `%TEMP%\lah-index-refresh-retry-787314fcf76248d5815c26511a67fbe6`.
- Concurrent foreground queries for the same resolved repository and fingerprint now share the existing bounded single-flight operation. The six-query regression previously opened the changed file six times and attempted six update batches. It now reads once, attempts one batch and returns the current symbol to every query. A failed shared refresh keeps the same stale and uncached fallback.
- Four paired Windows runs used eight concurrent requests against 64 changed Python files and a real SQLite CodeIndex. All ordered result pages agreed. Every control run hashed 512 files and called batch update eight times. Every coalesced run hashed 64 files and called batch update once. Median completion time was 1.10 seconds before the change and 0.30 seconds afterward. One coalesced sample took 2.16 seconds versus 1.12 seconds for its control. The input fingerprint was held constant by the fixture. First-query latency on the live endpoint remains open.
- The concurrency stage passed 136 Windows tests and 133 offline Debian tests. Three optional parser tests were skipped on Debian, and all 2,349 snapshot inputs remained stable. Evidence is in `%TEMP%\lah-foreground-index-flight-_rjp8nn3`.

- Fresh review quality and response-contract verification: `python -m pytest --no-cache -q tests/test_async_job_http_status_mapping.py tests/test_async_review_sla.py tests/test_audit_review_delivery_quality.py tests/test_review_diff_chunking.py tests/test_review_diff_output_budget.py tests/test_semantic_quality.py tests/test_t12_semantic_quality_contract.py` → **63 passed**. These are isolated tests, not a live model-quality benchmark.
- The same check confirms only delivery/retry policy in isolation. Queue age can still produce an intentional 503, and review quality/runtime behavior, restart recovery, and live endpoint acceptance remain open. Hub stays disabled; no service restart was performed.
- Fresh telemetry requery at the `7.0` tag timestamp (2026-11-23 05:37 CET) currently retains 17,283 events: 440 have `success=0`, 274 are marked degraded, and 751 meet the union of failure, HTTP error, or degraded. Counts overlap. HTTP p95/max are `/api/context/pack` 16.581/29.412 s (72), `/api/review/diff` 120.012/156.142 s (30), `/api/review` 29.911/29.911 s (14), and `/api/solve/repo` 11.276/17.976 s (6). This rebaseline supersedes earlier aggregate counts in older audit notes.

The missing/mismatched RAG index is consistent with the requested v8 vector reset; vectorless-path requeue is fixed, but re-embedding and live search quality remain unverified. Source uncertainty stays fail-closed; bounded result limits must remain visibly incomplete.

## RAG rebuild after v8 embedding reset — 2026-11-24

- Current persisted state has 31,719/31,719 RAG chunk vectors empty, 8,397 paths with vectorless chunks and no workspace embedding-space marker, and 2,273 preprocessor source rows still marked RAG-current. Twenty-three projects are waiting/error in the RAG phase; five retain embedding-related last errors. The service is disabled, so these counts are a stopped-state snapshot.
- Confirmed defect: `RAGStorageMixin.indexed_paths()` only checked vector payloads when a workspace embedding-space marker existed. V8 removes those markers with the old vectors, so vectorless files were incorrectly reported indexed and the preprocessor could skip rebuilding them. A regression test failed before the fix and passes now. The method treats NULL, empty and `'[]'` vectors as missing even without a stored marker.
- Fresh RAG/reset/index verification: 55 passed, 3 symlink-dependent skips; preprocessing RAG contracts: 36 passed. This enables the next service run to discover and requeue vectorless paths, but embedding regeneration and endpoint/runtime acceptance remain unverified while the service stays disabled.
- Fresh checkout checks after this change: `python -m compileall -q src tools tests`, `python tools/release_check.py --allow-installed`, and `git diff --check` all passed.

## Review model failure status — 2026-11-24

- Bounded debug traces show review HTTP 500s caused by two different conditions: the model reached the caller's output-token limit (`model_output_truncated`) and the local model returned an upstream HTTP 500 (`local_model_error`, including a repetition-limit abort). Neither is a Hub route crash.
- HTTP mapping now returns 422 for truncated model output and 502 for a non-retryable local model failure. Review-diff's segmented failure wrapper preserves an explicit status code. Truncation stays a failed, unusable answer; no partial review is advertised as complete.
- Test-first proof: both new mappings failed with 500 before the patch. Current review/status suites: 59 passed. This changes failure reporting and keeps quality fail-closed; it does not make a misconfigured small output budget or provider repetition succeed. Live endpoint behavior remains unverified while Hub is disabled.
- After this change, `compileall`, `release_check.py --allow-installed`, and `git diff --check` passed.

## Context-pack latency evidence — 2026-11-24

- Release-window telemetry has 73 successful `/api/context/pack` requests. p50 is 7.305 s, p95 16.581 s, maximum 29.411 s. The slowest samples spend virtually all measured time in handler execution; DB-wait is unmeasured/zero, serialization is under 0.5 ms.
- Trace recheck corrects the earlier mode attribution: the 16.823 s request was `mode=adaptive`, then degraded to `deterministic-fast-fallback` with 0 evidence and 12 estimated tokens. The fallback's deterministic result had `query_cache_hit=true`; telemetry attributes 16.679 s to the outer context retrieval. That does not support blaming the deterministic resolver itself. The adaptive stale-evidence path is the likely slow phase, but existing telemetry has no backend subphase timings to isolate its cause.
- Source inspection found avoidable repeat searches on adaptive cache misses: deterministic `related_paths()` and `CodeIndex.related_paths()` could query output already produced by the deterministic resolver. Both now accept reusable results, and `_hybrid_context_uncached()` passes the code-index result when supported. `context_pack()` also renders already-ranked symbols and skips a repeated search for an explicit empty symbol result. An isolated replay of the final fallback's old symbol builder took 47 ms and made 24 index calls, so that path does not explain the recorded 16.679 s. Fresh context/ranking/adaptive-retrieval suite: 138 passed, including direct and call-site reuse regressions. No live latency delta is claimed. Adaptive retrieval timing and live endpoint acceptance remain open.
- The same fresh adaptive-retrieval run exposed a pre-existing assertion for Czech query cleanup. Added `jak`, `najdu` and `přes` to generic lexical request words; the regression now passes while preserving `instalaci`, `local`, `hub` and `repo_search`.

## Bounded preprocessing candidates — 2026-11-24

- Offline trace `req_2a2bc44488c1` attributes 26.446 s to context preparation before optional backend timing; trace does not isolate individual preparation calls. Source inspection found `preprocessor.candidate_paths()` ran synchronously in this phase and could wait on preprocessing storage locks without respecting the adaptive backend deadline.
- Candidate lookup now runs in a dedicated single-worker lane with one-slot admission. The request waits only for the shared retrieval deadline; a timed-out/busy lookup is reported as degraded and cannot hold the request indefinitely. Admission remains occupied until the actual worker exits, preventing an unbounded queue behind a stuck lookup.
- Regression proof: the blocking-preprocessor test failed before the change and passes after. Fresh relevant adaptive/context ranking/preprocessor checks: **68 passed**. Compileall, `release_check.py --allow-installed`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- A broader combined selection ran 82 checks: 81 passed and one unrelated assertion failed in `tests/test_context_pack_budget_e2e.py::test_mcp_adaptive_context_pack_crosses_http_with_bounded_provenance[detail-on]` because the response omits diagnostic `response_budget.original_tokens`. This was not changed as part of the latency fix and needs separate reconciliation with the diagnostics privacy contract.
- The async fix bounds this candidate-search wait but does not prove it accounted for the full historical 26.446 s preparation interval. Hub remains disabled, so live endpoint latency delta and reindex acceptance remain unverified.

HF reports the repo public and LFS hash/size matching the local artifact. Repository defaults, all hardware preprocessing routes, the example config and current user config now select `qwen2.5-coder-prep-v4:3b`; Ollama first-use provisioning pins the public revision and verifies SHA-256 before registration. TOML parsing, anonymous HF HEAD, metadata and current Ollama model identity were verified. A separate clean-cache first-use run was not performed.
